# Code review: Glyphbound day 1 (read-only)

You are the second-opinion reviewer for **Glyphbound**, a first-person RPG shooter in Unreal Engine 5.9.0 (C++), made for a 4-day game jam. Do not modify any file. Do not build or run anything.

## Context
- Read `Design/ARCHITECTURE.md` first (short). Design is in `Design/GDD.md` (sections 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 matter most).
- Code: `Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/` (about 17,000 lines, written today by ten AI implementers in parallel against header contracts, then being compiled by another agent right now, so a missing include is NOT what we want from you).
- Data: `Glyphbound/Content/Data/*.json`, loaded by `Data/GBDataSubsystem.cpp` into the structs of `Data/GBDataTypes.h`.
- Engine source for checking APIs: `C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.9\Engine\Source`.

## What to look for (real defects only, in this priority order)
1. **Runtime bugs in the combat core**: `Combat/`, `Stats/`, `Weapons/`, `Spells/` (damage path, status effects, projectile collisions and lifetimes, ammo and reload state, cooldowns, mana spent / refunded wrongly, sustained spells never ending, time dilation left on after the QTE).
2. **Mismatches between data and code**: a stat, param, string key, id, enum value or class path used in a JSON file that the code never reads or spells differently (and the reverse: code reading a key no JSON defines, so it silently falls back to a default). Check ids across files (spells, weapons, items, status effects, enemies, encounters, sprites, skills modifier keys like `Spell.Fireball.ExplosionRadius`).
3. **Cross-module contract breaks**: one module assuming something another does not do (initialization order in `Player/GBPlayerCharacter.cpp` BeginPlay, who restores health on era travel, who saves checkpoints, who closes the death menu, delegate bindings never removed, objects kept alive only by raw pointers and collected by the GC, `TWeakObjectPtr` not checked).
4. **Things that work in the Editor but break in a packaged build**: editor-only APIs outside guards, assets loaded by path at runtime that the cook would not include (see `Glyphbound/Config/DefaultGame.ini`), transient textures, `LoadClass` paths.
5. **Logic errors in the QTE** (`QTE/GBQTELogic.cpp`, `QTE/GBQTEComponent.cpp`): scoring, time limit in real time, input routed while inactive.
6. **Violations of the project rule "no gameplay numbers in code"** only when it is a real balance value (damage, cost, cooldown, speed, duration) hardcoded where data should decide.

Do not report: style, naming, formatting, missing comments, micro-optimizations, hypothetical multiplayer issues (the game is single player), or anything about tests being too few.

## Output format
A numbered list, most severe first, at most 30 items. For each:
- `file:line` (or lines),
- what is wrong, in one or two sentences,
- why it matters when playing (what the player would see),
- the smallest fix you would make.
Finish with one short paragraph: the three things you would fix first, and anything you checked and found solid.

# Glyphbound: code architecture

> Written by the architect (Claude Code) on 2026-11-12. Source of truth for how the C++ is organized.
> Design lives in `Design/GDD.md`; this file says how the code implements it. Keep it short and current.

## Principles
1. **Data, not code.** Every gameplay number lives in `Glyphbound/Content/Data/*.json` and is read through `UGBDataSubsystem`. Code never hardcodes damage, costs, speeds, durations, sizes or timings. The JSON ships as loose files, so numbers can be tuned in a packaged build with a text editor (`gb.ReloadData` reloads while playing).
2. **One stat system.** A number is a *named stat*: base value from data, then modifiers. `UGBStatsComponent` evaluates `(Base + adds) * multipliers` (or an override). Skill-tree nodes, armor, status effects, air passives and the weapon enchant are all just groups of modifiers with a source name. Nothing else changes numbers.
3. **One damage path.** Everything that hurts goes through `UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(Target, FGBDamageInfo)`. Shields, resistances, teams, on-hit effects and kill credit hook in there once.
4. **Spells are data + a behavior.** A spell is a `FGBSpellDefinition` (params with `Base/Min/Max/ScaledBy`) plus the class of an `AGBSpellActor` behavior. The QTE result (power, reach) scales the params tagged for it. A new spell that fits an existing behavior is a JSON entry, no code.
5. **Shared pieces.** One projectile (`AGBProjectile`) for staff bolts, spell projectiles, enemy shots and grenades. One status-effect component for slows, burns, potions and buffs. One interact interface for portals, chests, pickups and the seal.
6. **Text in git.** No binary assets are required to run: input actions, the test arena, placeholder shapes and placeholder pixel sprites are all built in C++ from text. `Content/` is ignored by git except `Content/Data/`.
7. **No GAS.** Unreal's Gameplay Ability System was considered and rejected for the jam: it needs a lot of boilerplate and asset setup, and the game's needs (costs, cooldowns, timed effects, stat upgrades) are covered by principles 2-4 in a few hundred lines we fully control.
8. **PIE is human-only.** Agents build, run the pure-logic tests and package; a person plays.

## Module layout (`Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound`, one runtime module, prefix `GB`)
Includes are relative to the module root: `#include "Stats/GBStatsComponent.h"`.

| Folder | What | Key types |
|---|---|---|
| `Core/` | Shared enums, stat names, placeholders, game mode | `GBTypes.h`, `GBStatNames.h`, `GBPlaceholder.h`, `AGBGameMode`, `UGBGameInstance` |
| `Data/` | Definitions and the JSON loader | `GBDataTypes.h`, `UGBDataSubsystem` |
| `Stats/` | Stats + modifiers, health/mana | `UGBStatsComponent`, `UGBAttributeComponent` |
| `Combat/` | Damage, status effects, projectile | `UGBCombatLibrary`, `UGBStatusEffectComponent`, `AGBProjectile` |
| `Player/` | Character, controller, input | `AGBPlayerCharacter`, `AGBPlayerController`, `UGBInputSet` |
| `Weapons/` | Held items and slots | `UGBEquipmentComponent`, `AGBHeldItem`, `AGBStaff`, `AGBGun`, `AGBSword` |
| `Spells/` | Spellbook, cast resolution, behaviors | `UGBSpellbookComponent`, `FGBSpellCast`, `AGBSpellActor`, `AGBSpell_*` |
| `QTE/` | Quick-time event | `FGBQTESession`, `FGBGestureRecognizer`, `UGBQTEComponent` |
| `World/` | Eras, progress, interactables, test arena | `UGBEraSubsystem`, `UGBEraComponent`, `UGBProgressSubsystem`, `IGBInteractable`, `AGBPortal`, `AGBChest`, `AGBPickup`, `AGBCheckpointVolume`, `AGBTestArena` |
| `AI/` | Enemies and encounters | `AGBEnemyCharacter`, `AGBEncounter` |
| `Items/` | Inventory, consumables, armor, recipes | `UGBInventoryComponent` |
| `Progression/` | Skill trees | `UGBSkillTreeComponent` |
| `UI/` | Canvas HUD (two skins), menus, pixel sprites | `AGBHUD`, `UGBPixelSpriteLibrary` |
| `Tests/` | Pure-logic automation tests | `Glyphbound.<Area>.<Name>` |

## Data files (`Glyphbound/Content/Data`)
`Player.json`, `QTE.json`, `Game.json`, `Spells.json`, `Weapons.json`, `StatusEffects.json`, `Items.json` (items + recipes), `Enemies.json`, `Encounters.json`, `Skills.json`, `Sprites.json`. Schemas are the structs in `Data/GBDataTypes.h` (field name = JSON key). Stat and parameter names are the constants in `Core/GBStatNames.h`.

Modifier keys: an actor stat (`MoveSpeed`), `Weapon.<Id>.<Stat>`, `Weapon.All.<Stat>`, `Spell.<Id>.<Param>`, `Spell.All.<Param>`, `QTE.TimeDilation`...

Standard status effect ids (defined in `StatusEffects.json`, referenced from other files): `Burn`, `Shock`, `Slow`, `Chill`, `HealthPotion`, `ManaPotion`, `HealthInjection`, `Enchant_Fire`, `Enchant_Lightning`, `Enchant_Ice`.

## How the main flows run
- **Cast:** input -> `UGBSpellbookComponent::TryCastSlot` (checks staff in hand, known, cooldown, mana; spends mana) -> `UGBQTEComponent::StartQTE` (slow time, sequence = power, gesture = reach; or instant when off) -> `GBSpell::ResolveCast` (data -> QTE scaling -> modifiers) -> spawn the behavior actor -> it deals damage through `UGBCombatLibrary`.
- **Shoot:** input -> `AGBGun` (fire interval, magazine, spread, recoil, pellets; every number via `GetStat`) -> hitscan -> headshot and distance falloff math in `UGBCombatLibrary` -> `DealDamage`.
- **Era travel:** `AGBPortal::Interact` -> `UGBEraSubsystem::RequestTravel` (fade out, swap era for `UGBEraComponent` actors and `_Past` / `_Future` sub-levels, teleport, restore health/mana/ammo, save checkpoint, fade in).
- **Progress:** encounters set flags in `UGBProgressSubsystem` when cleared; `UGBEraComponent` reads flags, which is how era-gated zones and past-to-future unlocks are built. Death restores the last checkpoint snapshot; flags are never rolled back (enemies do not respawn).
- **Upgrades:** `UGBSkillTreeComponent::Unlock` adds the node's modifiers to the player's stats; weapons and spells pick them up on their next read.

## Conventions
- Game text in English. Code comments in English. ASCII only in source files.
- Native multicast delegates (C++ project; no Blueprint graphs needed).
- No custom collision channels: projectiles and queries use the default channels and filter by team in code.
- Pointers to definitions are not kept across frames (data can be reloaded): keep the id, look it up.
- Tests: only pure logic that is easy to break and hard to see by playing; each one must be able to fail; names `Glyphbound.<Area>.<Name>` where a name is never also a prefix of another.
- Build: `powershell -File Tools/Jam/Build-Jam.ps1` (Editor closed). One build at a time per machine.

## 0. Baseline
At its core this is a **classic first-person RPG plus a classic first-person shooter**. Anything not stated here behaves as players expect from those two genres (magazines and reload, ammo pickups, standard shooter gamepad layout, etc.). This document only lists what is specific to our game.

## 0.1 Technical direction
- **Engine:** Unreal Engine 5.9.0, pinned until submission. **Single player only.**
- **Architect autonomy:** nobody on the team is a programmer. **Every technical and engineering decision is the architect's**, taken without asking the owner: code architecture, C++ vs. Blueprints, plugins, tools, data formats, optimization. Decide, build, and log the decision and its reason in the dev log. When a design detail is missing, pick a reasonable provisional value, mark it provisional here, and keep going; ask the owner only when the game's vision would change.
- **Time first:** iteration speed matters more than elegance. Unused plugins off, Live Coding for `.cpp` changes, only the touched tests on each change, the full suite (under 30 s) once before the daily package. Rules in `CLAUDE.md` ("Tiempo de iteraciÃ³n").
- **Code (decided by the architect, 2026-11-12):** C++ only, one module, no Blueprint graphs; structure in `Design/ARCHITECTURE.md`. **Unreal's Gameplay Ability System is not used**: a small stat-and-modifier system, one damage function and one status-effect component cover the same needs with far less setup.
- **Tuning data (decided by the architect):** every gameplay number lives in text files, `Glyphbound/Content/Data/*.json` (spells, weapons, enemies, items, skills, player, QTE...). They ship as loose files: the owner can change numbers in a packaged build with a text editor, and `gb.ReloadData` in the console reloads them while playing. This replaces the data tables suggested earlier (git only carries text in this team, and AI agents edit text reliably).
- **Test level (decided by the architect):** the day-1 test arena is built in code on the engine's empty map, so no binary map asset is needed yet. Real levels come later from the level designers.
- **Placeholders first:** pixel art and 3D models are made by other team members on machines on the local network. **The architect never waits for art:** it uses quickly generated placeholders and swaps them later.
- **Placeholder numbers:** the architect puts provisional values (damage, mana, cooldowns, QTE length and time limitâ€¦) in data tables; the owner tunes them later.
- **PIE is human-only:** Play In Editor is always run by a person. AI agents do not run PIE; they compile, package and run tests, then ask a human to play-test. (Also in `CLAUDE.md`.)
- **Era travel:** a fade with an effect when crossing a portal; the transition hides loading the other era's version of the map.
- **Saving:** checkpoints only. No save to disk between sessions.
- **Size:** the jam page says "keep it under 1 GB" for desktop zips, but **bigger builds are allowed** if uploaded with butler (installed on CENTRAL) or Blendo Itch Uploader. The Official Rules PDF sets no size limit. So 1 GB is a target, not a hard limit.
- **NVIDIA RTX Award:** we intend to compete. It is the "best Render Royalty entry built in Unreal Engine" that uses at least one of: an NvRTX feature (RTX Mega Geometry, RTX Direct Illumination, ReSTIR GI, ReSTIR PT path tracing, LSS Hair), **DLSS 4.5 (Unreal Engine)**, or NVIDIA ACE; plus the NVIDIA questions at submission and listing the tool in "Tools you used".
- **Modifier rule (Official Rules Â§10):** a team can win **only one modifier** (Glass Box, Render Royalty, Best Teamâ€¦) unless no other eligible winner remains. We still enter all that apply.

## 1. One-line pitch
A fast first-person **shooter/RPG hybrid**: the player, a medieval mage, jumps through fixed portals between a **pixel-art medieval past** full of magic and a **3D cyberpunk future** full of guns, on the same map in two times, and wins by **mixing spells and firearms** rather than sticking to one style.

Working title: **TBD**. Project code name: **Glyphbound**.

## 2. Pillars
1. **Two eras, one fight.** Past = magic, pixel art. Future = guns, 3D cyberpunk. Both feed the same character.
2. **Combos over specialization.** Spells and guns can be used in both eras, and the game rewards combining them. Weapon and spell mechanics have synergies.
3. **Fast and dynamic combat:** spells, ranged spells, shooting, and melee once the elemental sword is unlocked.
4. **Magic feels crafted, not triggered:** casting uses a two-part slowed-time quick-time event that scales the spell (see Â§7).

## 3. Art rule (never broken)
- **Everything medieval, wizards and magic is pixel art (2D).**
- **Everything cyberpunk and futuristic is 3D.**
- **The rule follows the object, not the era.** A 3D gun stays 3D in the past; a pixel-art fireball stays pixel art in the future.
- **Characters take the style of the world they belong to.** An orc of the castle is a classic pixel-art fantasy orc (80s, 90s, early 2000s games). An orc of the future is a 3D, heavily armored sci-fi orc. They are different enemies, not the same orc in two styles.
- **The player is a medieval mage, so the player is always pixel art, hands included.** In first person, the hands are always pixel art, even when they hold a 3D gun.
- No exceptions.

### The pixel-art look
- **Visual reference:** retro first-person fantasy shooters with modern lighting (the owner's reference: *Wizordum*). Reference only, nothing copied.
- **Pixel-art environments are 3D geometry with low-resolution pixel textures** (nearest filtering, no smoothing), not flat 2D.
- **Pixel-art characters are sprites that always face the camera.** Target: **8 view angles**; fewer if time runs short.
- **Headshots on sprites:** the head is the top part of the sprite.

## 4. World and travel
- **One map, two times** (past and future). The player travels between them **only through predefined portals** placed on the map.
- **Era-gated zones:** some zones are reachable in one era and not in the other.
- **Cross-era unlocks:** some zones must be unlocked in the past so they open in the future (cause in the past, effect in the future).
- These interactions are few (short jam) but they exist and are part of level design.
- **Nothing is lost when changing era.** Health, mana, ammo, inventory, spells and weapons all carry over. Mana exists and works in the future; guns work in the past.
- **Era travel restores everything** (health, mana, ammo), a bit like the rest points of classic action RPGs. Portals are the safe points of the map.

- A third, separate map unlocks late: a **dimension between space and time** where the true final boss lives (see Â§11).

### Story
- The player is a **mage** who travels to the castle out of **curiosity**: he had heard it was a mysterious place.
- On reaching the **seal** in the castle he is **trapped** and thrown into the future.
- In the future he hears **a woman's voice asking for help**. That voice is his reason to fix the problem.
- **The twist:** the voice is **not the Valkyrie**. It is **the Entity**, the true final boss, pretending to be her.
- **The Entity:** a fusion of an **apocalyptic artificial intelligence** and a **ghost that embodies disease**. It is locked in the dimension between space and time and wants out. To escape it needs two things: someone to **open the portal** and a **physical body to possess**.
- **The Valkyrie:** someone the Entity **manipulated across time**. The plan failed, and she ended up **trapped in both eras** (runes in the past, cryogenic tube in the future).
- **What the player does:** defeats the Valkyrie in both eras, gathers what is needed, and opens the portal: **exactly the Entity's plan**. The player walks in with the body it wants to possess. The only thing the Entity did not plan for: **the player beats it**.
- Cutscenes: **few and short**, each one written for its situation.

### Opening sequence
1. The player starts in the **medieval castle** (past) with a **staff and the starting spells** (Â§6).
2. Kills a **small group of pixel-art orcs**.
3. **Interacts with the seal** â†’ **short cutscene #1** (trapped, sent forward in time).
4. Appears in the **future**.
5. Fights **3D armed orcs**. One of the first ones **drops the first gun**.
6. The player **picks up the gun** â†’ **short cutscene #2**. The player **cannot progress** without picking it up.
7. Back to the **castle**.
8. **Elemental zones**, in the order the player chooses (Â§6).
9. **Valkyrie, past.**
10. **Valkyrie, future.**
11. **Final portal** â†’ the Entity (Â§11).

Steps 7â€“11 are **provisional** (owner, 2026-11-12).

## 5. Player progression
- **No character levels / no XP leveling.**
- Progression is through **skill trees**: one for spells and one for weapons (see Â§13 for their look).
  - **Spell tree:** upgrades spells.
  - **Weapon tree:** damage, fire rate, **recoil** (better aim), **damage by distance** (shotgun stronger up close, rifle stronger far away), **headshot damage**.
- **One shared pool of skill points** for both trees.
- **Skill points** are given at specific points, mainly for defeating enemies:
  - defeating each **sub-boss**;
  - defeating each **boss**;
  - **clearing a wave** of enemies.

## 6. Magic (4 elements + weapon enchant)
Each element has 3 entries. Air is passives only; the other three are active spells.

| Element | 1 | 2 | 3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Fire** | Fireball (projectile) | Fire wall | Flamethrower (sustained DPS) |
| **Lightning** | Lightning bolt (projectile) | Lightning rune trap on the ground; triggers when an enemy walks over it | Static-electricity ball: moves and damages every enemy around it |
| **Ice** | Ice shield (held block, like a first-person RPG shield, but with a cooldown and limits) | Ice wall | Ice floor: slows enemies, light crowd control |
| **Air** (passives) | Double jump | Improved dash | Increased movement speed |

### Spell loadout
- The player has **5 spells equipped** at a time and can **equip and unequip** them freely.
- **Air passives are always active**: they are not equipped and do not take a slot.
- **Starting spells:** Fireball (very early tier), Lightning bolt (early tier), Ice wall.

### Learning the other spells: elemental zones
- The **medieval castle** has **elemental zones**, one per element.
- Each zone has a **challenge**: a wave, a sub-boss or a boss.
- After the challenge the player gets the element's **knowledge** (from a chest, a book or similar) and **unlocks the missing spells of that element** (fire: fire wall + flamethrower; lightning: rune trap + static ball; ice: ice shield + ice floor).
- **Air passives** are learned the same way, in the air zone.
- The spell tree then upgrades what the player knows.
- **Spells can only be cast with the staff in hand.** With a gun in hand, the only spell available is the weapon enchant. To cast, the player switches to the staff mid-fight.
- Keys: see Â§9.

### Weapon enchant (always equipped)
- A spell that **cannot be unequipped**.
- **Only usable with a gun in hand** (it enchants the gun).
- Animation: the **left hand draws a magic glyph over the weapon held in the right hand**.
- Effect: the weapon is **temporarily buffed with magic** of one element.
- **Element:** chosen by the player when equipping the spell.
- **Uses the QTE** like every other spell (Â§7).
- **Does not work on the elemental sword.**
- Duration, cost and exact buff per element: **TBD**.

### General
- Spells cost **mana**.
- **Health and mana regenerate on their own** (potions and the injection speed it up). **No stamina.**
- Numbers for each spell (damage, cost, cooldowns, upgrade tiers): **TBD**, given later.
- The ice shield's exact limits (duration, damage absorbed, break behavior): **TBD**.

## 7. Spell-casting quick-time event
**Status:** wanted for every spell, the enchant included; still a trial for the 4-day schedule, so it must be possible to switch it off (spells then cast at a fixed default strength).

Purpose: make magic feel more elaborate than pulling a trigger.

### Flow
1. Player casts. **Time slows** by about **90%** (world at ~10% speed). Inspired by the slowed time some games use while the player picks combat hacks.
2. **Part 1 â€” button sequence â†’ POWER.** Random sequence.
   - Keyboard & mouse: movement keys (W, A, S, D).
   - Gamepad: the four face buttons (A/B/X/Y or Cross/Circle/Square/Triangle).
3. **Part 2 â€” gesture â†’ REACH.**
   - Mouse: left-to-right, top-to-bottom, a circle, etc.
   - Gamepad: the right stick.
4. Spell fires with its parameters scaled by the two results; time returns to normal.

### Grading and cost
- Each step counts on its own: every mistake lowers the result of its part.
- **The spell is cast if the player gets at least one step right.** It fails only if **every** step is missed.
- **Mana is always spent**, even when the spell fails.

### What each part scales
| Spell | Part 1 (power) | Part 2 (reach) |
|---|---|---|
| Fireball | Damage | Blast radius / range |
| Ice wall | Duration and wall health | Width and height |
| Ice shield | Duration and block effectiveness (which part scales which: **TBD**) | |
| Others | **TBD**, following the same idea: part 1 = strength, part 2 = size/area/range |

### Requirements for the architect
- **Every spell parameter is data, not code**, and every one can be tagged as scaled by part 1, part 2, or neither. Each spell defines its own min/max per parameter.
- The **time-slow amount is configurable** and can be modified by gear (e.g. some armor). Armor/gear as a system is otherwise **TBD**.
- **The camera is locked during the QTE** (the mouse / right stick draws the gesture).
- **The player keeps moving during the QTE, in the direction they were already moving** (the movement keys are busy with part 1, so the last movement input is held).
- **QTE time limit: 3 seconds** for the **whole QTE** (both parts together), in real time (provisional value in data).

## 8. Weapons and items

### Held slots
- The player carries **one gun and one staff.** Never two guns or two staffs.
- Once obtained, the **elemental sword is a special third slot.**
- Switch with the **mouse wheel or Alt**; on gamepad, with a **D-pad arrow** (which one: **TBD**).
- Staff in hand: basic magic attack + the 5 spells. Gun in hand: shoot + aim + reload + the enchant.

### Staffs (medieval, pixel art)
- With a staff in hand, **primary fire = basic magic attack**. The attack **depends on which staff** the player holds, so there is more than one staff.
- Staffs are found in **chests** around the map.
- Staff list and what each basic attack does: **TBD**.

### Guns (cyberpunk, 3D)
- Pistol, shotgun, assault rifle.
- The **first gun** is dropped by one of the first armed orcs in the future (Â§4). The **shotgun and the rifle** are dropped later by enemies, picked up from the floor.
- With a gun in hand: **primary fire = shoot**, **secondary = aim**.
- **Magazines and reload**, like any shooter.
- **Ammo works like classic tactical shooters:** each gun has a fixed number of spare magazines, and each magazine a fixed number of bullets.
- **All ammo refills completely every time the player travels between eras.**
- Between refills, ammo is **picked up from the floor** where enemies drop it.
- Level design keeps ammo tight: never infinite, never starving.
- **Electric grenade** (throwable).

### Armor
- Found in **chests**, rarely.
- **One armor slot = one full armor.** No separate helmet, arms or chest pieces.
- Known effect: some armor changes the QTE time-slow amount (Â§7). Other stats: **TBD**.

### Chests
- Contain **staffs**, and rarely **armor**. Other contents: **TBD**.

### Melee
- **No melee before the elemental sword** (Â§11).

### Consumables
| Item | Era | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| Health potion | Past | Heals a set amount **over time** |
| Mana potion | Past | Restores a set amount of mana **over time** |
| Electric grenade | Future | Throwable, electric damage |
| Health injection | Future | Heals **instantly**, but less than the potion |

### Synergies
Weapon mechanics and spell mechanics combine. The weapon enchant (Â§6) is the first confirmed one. Other synergies (e.g. shooting through a fire wall, electric grenade on an ice floor) are **TBD**.

## 9. Controls

| Action | Keyboard & mouse | Gamepad |
|---|---|---|
| Move | W A S D | Left stick |
| Look | Mouse | Right stick |
| Primary attack (staff: basic magic attack; gun: shoot) | Left click | R2 / RT |
| Aim (gun in hand) | Right click | L2 / LT |
| Spell slot 1 (staff in hand) | Right click | L1 / LB |
| Spell slots 2â€“5 (staff in hand) | 1, 2, 3, 4 | Hold L2 / LT + Cross, Circle, Square, Triangle (A, B, X, Y) |
| Reload (gun) | R | Standard shooter layout |
| Weapon enchant (gun in hand only) | T (R is reload) | L1 / LB (free with a gun in hand) |
| Switch staff â†” gun (â†” sword) | Mouse wheel or Alt | D-pad up |
| Jump | Space | Cross (A) |
| Dash | Ctrl | Circle (B) |
| Quick-use item (grenade / potion / injection share one slot) | F | R1 / RB |
| Cycle the quick-use item | Shift | D-pad left / right |
| Interact (chests, portals, objects) | G | Triangle (Y) |
| QTE part 1 | W A S D | Face buttons |
| QTE part 2 | Mouse gesture | Right stick |
| Inventory | Tab | Options / View |
| Skill trees | K (also a tab inside the inventory) | From the inventory |
| Pause | Esc | Start / Menu |

Gun controls on gamepad follow the standard shooter layout.

Right click / L1 change meaning with the held weapon and never clash: staff = spell slot 1; gun = aim (right click) and enchant (L1). L2 means "spell modifier" with the staff and "aim" with a gun.

No sprint (the air speed passive covers movement speed).

## 10. Enemies and encounters
- **Enemy waves (hordes).** Clearing a wave gives skill points.
- **A couple of sub-bosses.**
- **Two bosses** (the Valkyrie in each era, Â§11).
- **The true final boss.**
- Known enemies: **orcs** (past) and **armed orcs** (future). Other types and behavior: **TBD**. Their art follows Â§3 (medieval = pixel art, cyberpunk = 3D).
- Enemies **drop ammo** on the floor.
- **Enemies do not respawn.** A cleared zone stays cleared, also after era travel.

### Death and difficulty
- On death the player **returns to the last checkpoint**. Checkpoints are **automatic**.
- **Difficulty: hard**, the only one for now. A separate normal mode is possible later (for example, the spell QTE only in hard); out of scope until the owner says otherwise.
- No target length: we build as much as the time allows.

## 11. Bosses and endgame
**The Valkyrie (two bosses, one in each era).**
- **Past:** trapped inside magic runes in the medieval castle.
- **Future:** trapped in a cryogenic tube.
- The player must **free her and defeat her in both eras.**

Rewards:
| Defeated in | Drops |
|---|---|
| Past (castle) | **Elemental stones/gems** |
| Future | **A weapon hilt** |
| Both fights | One **quest item** each (what they are: **TBD**) |

- **Elemental sword:** in the inventory, the player combines the hilt with a gem and gets an elemental sword that comes out of the hilt. The player can switch the sword to the other elements from the inventory. It is the only melee weapon. The weapon enchant does not apply to it.
  - Art (Â§3): the **hilt is 3D** (it comes from the future), the **blade is pixel art** (it is magic).
  - HUD: **medieval** (provisional).
- **Final portal:** combining the two quest items opens a portal to a separate map, a dimension between space and time, where the player fights the **true final boss**.
- **True final boss: the Entity** (story in Â§4). A **mix of both art styles**, which fits what it is (suggestion, not decided: the AI half in 3D, the ghost half in pixel art). Undecided how: two phases with different styles, or one character in both styles at once, or something else (**TBD**).
- This happens in mid/late game.

## 12. Inventory
Needed for: consumables, equipping/unequipping spells, choosing the enchant element, combining hilt + gem, switching the sword's element, combining the two quest items. Layout and limits **TBD**.

## 13. UI: two styles
The UI changes with what the player holds:

| Holding | HUD style |
|---|---|
| Staff (magic) | **1990s first-person RPG** |
| Gun | **Modern cyberpunk** |
| Elemental sword | Medieval / 90s RPG (provisional) |

- **Skill trees** follow the same split: the spell tree has its own 90s-RPG look, the weapon tree its own cyberpunk look.
- The HUD switches when the player switches weapon. The inventory's look is **TBD**.

## 14. Theme fit (DIRECTOR'S CUT)
Our reading (owner, 2026-11-12): the theme is effectively **open**; what the organizers ask for is a game with an **author's signature**, a personal vision. We answer it with the game's own identity:
- the hard art split (pixel-art magic vs. 3D cyberpunk) that follows each object and never breaks, with a pixel-art mage holding 3D guns;
- a UI that also changes style with what the player holds;
- magic that has to be performed (the QTE) rather than triggered;
- a combat style that rewards mixing the two eras.

The dev log shows the directing side too: how we direct the AI agents, and every scope decision ("what made the final cut"). Any feature we drop gets logged as a cut, with the reason.

## 15. Scope for the jam (proposal, to discuss)
The jam is 4 days and we package a playable build every day. Suggested order so that every day ends with something playable:

| Day | Target |
|---|---|
| 1 (Nov 12) | First-person controller (move, dash, double jump), pixel-art hands, one staff, one gun, one spell with data-driven parameters, mana/health, one portal swapping eras in a test map. Packaged. |
| 2 (Nov 13) | Rest of guns + spells, 5-slot loadout, weapon enchant, both HUD styles, basic enemies and one wave, consumables, QTE first version. |
| 3 (Nov 14) | Sub-bosses, Valkyrie fights, inventory + elemental sword, skill trees, era-gated zones. |
| 4 (Nov 15) | Final boss arena, polish, audio, video, submission (deadline 20:59 EST). |

## 16. For the architect: systems to design
Not decisions, just the list of systems the design implies:
- Player controller: movement, double jump, dash, speed modifiers (air passives).
- Health, mana, damage, status effects (slow, heal/mana over time, instant heal, temporary weapon buff).
- Ability system shared by spells, staff attacks, guns and grenade (cooldowns, costs, upgrades from the skill trees). Unreal's Gameplay Ability System is one candidate; the architect decides.
- **Data-driven spell definitions:** every parameter configurable, with min/max and a tag saying whether QTE part 1 (power) or part 2 (reach) scales it.
- 5-slot spell loadout + the fixed enchant slot; equip/unequip.
- Spell QTE: input sequence (KB/M and gamepad), gesture recognition (mouse and right stick), per-step grading, global time dilation as a configurable, gear-modifiable value; can be disabled.
- Held items: staffs (basic attack per staff), guns (shoot, aim), electric grenade, enchant buff, melee sword with swappable element.
- Spawned spell actors: walls, floor zone, ground trap, moving damage ball, sustained flamethrower.
- Era system: same map in two times, portals, era-gated zones, past actions that open future zones, player state kept across eras.
- **Rendering of the art rule per object:** pixel-art sprites (player hands, medieval props, enemies, spells) and 3D meshes (guns, cyberpunk world) in the same first-person view.
- Two UI skins (90s RPG, modern cyberpunk) for HUD and skill trees, switched by the held item.
- Encounters: waves, sub-bosses, bosses (free + fight + drops), skill point rewards.
- Inventory with item combining; two skill trees.
- Final portal and separate final-boss map.
- Input for keyboard & mouse and gamepad (layout in Â§9).

## 18. Provisional rules and numbers picked by the architect (2026-11-12)
The owner asked the architect not to stop for missing design details. Everything below was chosen so the prototype could be built; **all of it is provisional** and waits for the owner's word. Numbers are in `Glyphbound/Content/Data/*.json` and can be changed there without touching code.

### Rules
- **Double jump on day 1:** the player starts knowing the air passive "Double jump" so it can be tested now. In the real game it is learned in the air zone (Â§6); remove it from `Player.json` `StartingSpells` then.
- **Dash:** available from the start (the air passive only improves it). It goes in the movement direction (forward when standing still), works in the air, gives no invulnerability, and does not refill jumps.
- **Swapping weapons:** picking up a gun or a staff replaces the one held; the old one is left on the floor. A gun that is picked up comes fully loaded.
- **Reload:** the bullets left in the old magazine are lost (classic tactical style). A gun reloads by itself when its magazine runs dry. Ammo pickups give one spare magazine, up to the gun's maximum.
- **Sustained spells (flamethrower, ice shield):** after the QTE they run for their Duration, or until the same spell key is pressed again, the weapon is switched, or the player dies. Mana is paid once, at the start.
- **Ice shield QTE:** part 1 (power) scales its duration and health; part 2 (reach) scales how much frontal damage it blocks.
- **Weapon enchant QTE:** part 1 scales how long the buff lasts; part 2 scales how strong it is. Only one enchant at a time. The enchanted gun deals the element's damage type and applies its effect on hit (fire burns, lightning shocks, ice chills).
- **Spell walls:** the fire wall does not block; it burns enemies that touch it. The ice wall blocks movement and shots and has health; anyone's shots can break it, the player's included. Enemies do not attack walls on purpose yet.
- **QTE:** a wrong key consumes its step. A gesture is judged when the hand stops for a moment or the stroke is clearly complete. If both parts are fully missed the spell fizzles, and the mana is still spent and the cooldown still starts.
- **New spells** go to the first free loadout slot.
- **Elemental sword:** the hilt and the gems are not consumed when forging, so the player re-forges from the inventory to switch element.
- **Enemies:** once hit, an enemy chases the player from any distance. Enemies of the other era are frozen and hidden, not simulated.
- **Encounters:** if the player dies during one, its living enemies are removed and it starts again from wave 1 on return. Progress flags are never rolled back.
- **Checkpoints:** saved at the start, on every era travel, on entering a checkpoint zone and when an encounter is cleared. Death brings back the player's state as it was at the checkpoint (health, mana, ammo, items, spells, skills).
- **Consumables** can be used at full health or mana (they are wasted).
- **Art rule on pickups:** cyberpunk items (injection, grenade, magazine, sword hilt) lie on the floor as 3D shapes; medieval ones (potions, gems, books, staffs) as pixel-art sprites.

### Numbers (first pass, all in the JSON files)
- **Player:** 100 health (regenerates 2/s after 5 s without damage), 100 mana (6/s after 1.5 s), speed 750, dash 7 m in 0.18 s every 1.2 s.
- **QTE:** 4 keys, 1 gesture, 3 s in real time, world at 10% speed.
- **Staff:** Apprentice staff 12 damage every 0.35 s, no mana cost. Ember staff 22 fire damage every 0.6 s and burns.
- **Guns:** pistol 18 damage, 12 rounds, 4 spare magazines; shotgun 8 pellets of 10, 6 rounds, strong up close; rifle automatic, 14 damage, 30 rounds, stronger far away. Headshots x2 (shotgun x1.5).
- **Enemies:** past orc 60 health, melee 12; future orc 70 health, ranged 9; brute (sub-boss placeholder) 320 health, melee 28.
- **Status effects:** burn 32 damage over 4 s; slow 50% for 4 s; chill 30% for 3 s; shock 40% for 1.5 s; health potion 60 over 10 s; injection 35 at once; enchant +25% gun damage for 20 s.
- **Skill trees:** 6 nodes each, 1-2 points per rank, up to 3 ranks.
- Every spell number in `Spells.json`.

#include "Combat/GBCombatLibrary.h"

#include "Combat/GBStatusEffectComponent.h"
#include "Core/GBStatNames.h"
#include "Stats/GBAttributeComponent.h"
#include "Stats/GBStatsComponent.h"
#include "Engine/Engine.h"
#include "Engine/OverlapResult.h"
#include "Engine/World.h"
#include "CollisionQueryParams.h"

float UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(AActor* Target, const FGBDamageInfo& Info, bool bAllowFriendlyFire)
{
	if (!IsValid(Target))
	{
		return 0.f;
	}

	UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(Target);
	if (!Attributes)
	{
		return 0.f;
	}

	AActor* DamageInstigator = Info.Instigator.Get();
	if (!IsValid(DamageInstigator))
	{
		DamageInstigator = nullptr;
	}

	// A null instigator or a Neutral team on either side always damages; otherwise only hostile teams do.
	if (!bAllowFriendlyFire && DamageInstigator)
	{
		const EGBTeam InstigatorTeam = GetTeam(DamageInstigator);
		const EGBTeam TargetTeam = Attributes->Team;
		if (InstigatorTeam != EGBTeam::Neutral && TargetTeam != EGBTeam::Neutral && !AreTeamsHostile(InstigatorTeam, TargetTeam))
		{
			return 0.f;
		}
	}

	FGBDamageInfo FinalInfo = Info;
	FinalInfo.Instigator = DamageInstigator;
	if (DamageInstigator)
	{
		if (const UGBStatsComponent* InstigatorStats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(DamageInstigator))
		{
			FinalInfo.Amount *= InstigatorStats->GetStat(GBStat::DamageDealt, 1.f);
		}
	}

	if (FinalInfo.Amount <= 0.f)
	{
		return 0.f;
	}

	const float Dealt = Attributes->ApplyDamage(FinalInfo);

	if (Dealt > 0.f && !FinalInfo.OnHitEffect.IsNone())
	{
		if (UGBStatusEffectComponent* Effects = UGBStatusEffectComponent::Find(Target))
		{
			Effects->ApplyEffect(FinalInfo.OnHitEffect, DamageInstigator, FinalInfo.EffectDurationScale, FinalInfo.EffectStrengthScale);
		}
	}

	return Dealt;
}

int32 UGBCombatLibrary::DealRadialDamage(const UObject* WorldContextObject, const FVector& Origin, float Radius, const FGBDamageInfo& Info,
	TConstArrayView<AActor*> IgnoreActors, bool bLinearFalloff)
{
	if (Radius <= 0.f || !GEngine)
	{
		return 0;
	}

	UWorld* World = GEngine->GetWorldFromContextObject(WorldContextObject, EGetWorldErrorMode::LogAndReturnNull);
	if (!World)
	{
		return 0;
	}

	FCollisionObjectQueryParams ObjectParams;
	ObjectParams.AddObjectTypesToQuery(ECC_Pawn);
	ObjectParams.AddObjectTypesToQuery(ECC_WorldDynamic);

	FCollisionQueryParams QueryParams(SCENE_QUERY_STAT(GBRadialDamage), false);
	for (AActor* Ignored : IgnoreActors)
	{
		if (Ignored)
		{
			QueryParams.AddIgnoredActor(Ignored);
		}
	}

	TArray<FOverlapResult> Overlaps;
	World->OverlapMultiByObjectType(Overlaps, Origin, FQuat::Identity, ObjectParams, FCollisionShape::MakeSphere(Radius), QueryParams);

	AActor* DamageInstigator = Info.Instigator.Get();
	if (!IsValid(DamageInstigator))
	{
		DamageInstigator = nullptr;
	}

	TSet<AActor*> Visited;
	int32 DamagedCount = 0;

	for (const FOverlapResult& Overlap : Overlaps)
	{
		AActor* Target = Overlap.GetActor();
		if (!IsValid(Target) || Visited.Contains(Target))
		{
			continue;
		}
		Visited.Add(Target);

		if (IgnoreActors.Contains(Target) || !UGBAttributeComponent::Find(Target))
		{
			continue;
		}

		// With no instigator (traps, world hazards) everything with attributes is a target.
		if (DamageInstigator && !AreHostile(DamageInstigator, Target))
		{
			continue;
		}

		const FVector TargetLocation = Target->GetActorLocation();
		const float Distance = FVector::Dist(TargetLocation, Origin);
		if (Distance > Radius)
		{
			continue;
		}

		FGBDamageInfo TargetInfo = Info;
		TargetInfo.HitLocation = TargetLocation;
		TargetInfo.HitDirection = (TargetLocation - Origin).GetSafeNormal();
		if (bLinearFalloff)
		{
			TargetInfo.Amount *= FMath::Clamp(1.f - Distance / Radius, 0.f, 1.f);
		}

		if (DealDamage(Target, TargetInfo) > 0.f)
		{
			++DamagedCount;
		}
	}

	return DamagedCount;
}

EGBTeam UGBCombatLibrary::GetTeam(const AActor* Actor)
{
	const UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(Actor);
	return Attributes ? Attributes->Team : EGBTeam::Neutral;
}

bool UGBCombatLibrary::AreHostile(const AActor* A, const AActor* B)
{
	if (!A || !B)
	{
		return false;
	}
	return AreTeamsHostile(GetTeam(A), GetTeam(B));
}

bool UGBCombatLibrary::AreTeamsHostile(EGBTeam A, EGBTeam B)
{
	return (A == EGBTeam::Player && B == EGBTeam::Enemy) || (A == EGBTeam::Enemy && B == EGBTeam::Player);
}

bool UGBCombatLibrary::IsAlive(const AActor* Actor)
{
	if (!IsValid(Actor))
	{
		return false;
	}
	const UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(Actor);
	return !Attributes || !Attributes->IsDead();
}

float UGBCombatLibrary::DistanceFalloffMultiplier(float Distance, float FalloffStart, float FalloffEnd, float NearMult, float FarMult)
{
	if (Distance <= FalloffStart)
	{
		return NearMult;
	}
	if (FalloffEnd <= FalloffStart || Distance >= FalloffEnd)
	{
		return FarMult;
	}
	const float Alpha = (Distance - FalloffStart) / (FalloffEnd - FalloffStart);
	return FMath::Lerp(NearMult, FarMult, Alpha);
}

bool UGBCombatLibrary::IsHeadshot(float HitZ, float FeetZ, float BodyHeight, float HeadFraction)
{
	if (HeadFraction <= 0.f || BodyHeight <= 0.f)
	{
		return false;
	}
	const float HeadLineZ = FeetZ + BodyHeight * (1.f - FMath::Min(HeadFraction, 1.f));
	return HitZ >= HeadLineZ;
}

FVector UGBCombatLibrary::ApplySpread(const FVector& Direction, float SpreadHalfAngleDegrees, FRandomStream& Random)
{
	const FVector Dir = Direction.GetSafeNormal();
	if (Dir.IsNearlyZero() || SpreadHalfAngleDegrees <= 0.f)
	{
		return Dir;
	}
	return Random.VRandCone(Dir, FMath::DegreesToRadians(SpreadHalfAngleDegrees));
}
#include "Combat/GBProjectile.h"

#include "AI/GBEnemyCharacter.h"
#include "Combat/GBCombatLibrary.h"
#include "Core/GBPlaceholder.h"
#include "Stats/GBAttributeComponent.h"
#include "UI/GBPixelSpriteLibrary.h"
#include "Components/BillboardComponent.h"
#include "Components/SphereComponent.h"
#include "Components/StaticMeshComponent.h"
#include "Engine/World.h"
#include "GameFramework/Pawn.h"
#include "GameFramework/ProjectileMovementComponent.h"

AGBProjectile::AGBProjectile()
{
	PrimaryActorTick.bCanEverTick = true;

	Collision = CreateDefaultSubobject<USphereComponent>(TEXT("Collision"));
	Collision->SetCollisionEnabled(ECollisionEnabled::QueryOnly);
	Collision->SetCollisionObjectType(ECC_WorldDynamic);
	Collision->SetCollisionResponseToAllChannels(ECR_Ignore);
	Collision->SetCollisionResponseToChannel(ECC_Pawn, ECR_Overlap);
	Collision->SetCollisionResponseToChannel(ECC_WorldDynamic, ECR_Overlap);
	Collision->SetCollisionResponseToChannel(ECC_WorldStatic, ECR_Block);
	Collision->SetGenerateOverlapEvents(true);
	Collision->SetCanEverAffectNavigation(false);
	Collision->OnComponentHit.AddDynamic(this, &AGBProjectile::OnCollisionHit);
	Collision->OnComponentBeginOverlap.AddDynamic(this, &AGBProjectile::OnCollisionOverlap);
	RootComponent = Collision;

	Movement = CreateDefaultSubobject<UProjectileMovementComponent>(TEXT("Movement"));
	Movement->UpdatedComponent = Collision;
	Movement->bRotationFollowsVelocity = true;
	// The launch velocity is given in world space by InitProjectile.
	Movement->bInitialVelocityInLocalSpace = false;
	Movement->bShouldBounce = false;
	Movement->ProjectileGravityScale = 0.f;

	Sprite = CreateDefaultSubobject<UBillboardComponent>(TEXT("Sprite"));
	Sprite->SetupAttachment(Collision);

	Mesh = CreateDefaultSubobject<UStaticMeshComponent>(TEXT("Mesh"));
	Mesh->SetupAttachment(Collision);
	Mesh->SetCollisionEnabled(ECollisionEnabled::NoCollision);
	Mesh->SetGenerateOverlapEvents(false);
	Mesh->SetCastShadow(false);
	Mesh->SetCanEverAffectNavigation(false);
}

AGBProjectile* AGBProjectile::SpawnProjectile(UWorld* World, const FGBProjectileConfig& InConfig, const FVector& Origin, const FVector& Direction, AActor* InInstigator)
{
	if (!World)
	{
		return nullptr;
	}

	const FVector LaunchDirection = Direction.IsNearlyZero() ? FVector::ForwardVector : Direction.GetSafeNormal();
	const FTransform SpawnTransform(LaunchDirection.Rotation(), Origin);

	AGBProjectile* Projectile = World->SpawnActorDeferred<AGBProjectile>(AGBProjectile::StaticClass(), SpawnTransform, InInstigator,
		Cast<APawn>(InInstigator), ESpawnActorCollisionHandlingMethod::AlwaysSpawn);
	if (!Projectile)
	{
		UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("Projectile: spawn failed"));
		return nullptr;
	}

	Projectile->InitProjectile(InConfig, LaunchDirection, InInstigator);
	Projectile->FinishSpawning(SpawnTransform);

	if (IsValid(InInstigator) && IsValid(Projectile))
	{
		Projectile->Collision->IgnoreActorWhenMoving(InInstigator, true);
		if (UPrimitiveComponent* InstigatorRoot = Cast<UPrimitiveComponent>(InInstigator->GetRootComponent()))
		{
			InstigatorRoot->IgnoreActorWhenMoving(Projectile, true);
		}
	}

	return IsValid(Projectile) ? Projectile : nullptr;
}

void AGBProjectile::InitProjectile(const FGBProjectileConfig& InConfig, const FVector& Direction, AActor* InInstigator)
{
	Config = InConfig;
	if (InInstigator)
	{
		Config.Damage.Instigator = InInstigator;
	}
	Config.Damage.Causer = this;
	StartLocation = GetActorLocation();

	const float Radius = FMath::Max(Config.Radius, 1.f);
	Collision->SetSphereRadius(Radius);

	Movement->InitialSpeed = Config.Speed;
	Movement->MaxSpeed = Config.Speed;
	Movement->ProjectileGravityScale = Config.GravityScale;
	Movement->bShouldBounce = Config.FuseTime > 0.f;
	Movement->Velocity = Direction.GetSafeNormal() * Config.Speed;

	const float VisualSize = Radius * 2.f * FMath::Max(Config.VisualScale, 0.01f);
	if (Config.bPixelArt)
	{
		Mesh->SetVisibility(false);
		if (UGBPixelSpriteLibrary* SpriteLibrary = UGBPixelSpriteLibrary::Get(this))
		{
			SpriteLibrary->SetupBillboard(Sprite, Config.SpriteId, VisualSize);
		}
		else
		{
			UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("Projectile: no pixel sprite library; sprite '%s' not shown"), *Config.SpriteId.ToString());
		}
	}
	else
	{
		Sprite->SetVisibility(false);
		Sprite->SetHiddenInGame(true);
		GBPlaceholder::SetupShape(Mesh, EGBShape::Sphere, FVector(VisualSize), Config.Color, /*bGlow*/ true);
	}
}

void AGBProjectile::BeginPlay()
{
	Super::BeginPlay();
}

void AGBProjectile::EndPlay(const EEndPlayReason::Type EndPlayReason)
{
	// Undo the instigator's ignore entry so its list does not grow with every shot.
	AActor* ProjectileInstigator = Config.Damage.Instigator.Get();
	if (IsValid(ProjectileInstigator))
	{
		if (UPrimitiveComponent* InstigatorRoot = Cast<UPrimitiveComponent>(ProjectileInstigator->GetRootComponent()))
		{
			InstigatorRoot->IgnoreActorWhenMoving(this, false);
		}
	}

	Super::EndPlay(EndPlayReason);
}

void AGBProjectile::Tick(float DeltaSeconds)
{
	Super::Tick(DeltaSeconds);

	if (bFinished)
	{
		return;
	}

	const FVector Location = GetActorLocation();
	const float Age = GetGameTimeSinceCreation();
	const bool bOutOfRange = Config.MaxRange > 0.f && FVector::Dist(StartLocation, Location) >= Config.MaxRange;
	const bool bTooOld = Config.MaxLifetime > 0.f && Age > Config.MaxLifetime;
	const bool bFuseDone = Config.FuseTime > 0.f && Age >= Config.FuseTime;

	if (bOutOfRange || bTooOld || bFuseDone)
	{
		if (Config.ExplosionRadius > 0.f)
		{
			Explode(Location);
		}
		Finish(nullptr, Location);
	}
}

bool AGBProjectile::IsValidTarget(const AActor* Other) const
{
	if (!IsValid(Other) || Other == this || Other->IsA<AGBProjectile>())
	{
		return false;
	}

	const AActor* ProjectileInstigator = Config.Damage.Instigator.Get();
	if (Other == ProjectileInstigator)
	{
		return false;
	}

	if (!UGBAttributeComponent::Find(Other) || !UGBCombatLibrary::IsAlive(Other))
	{
		return false;
	}

	// No instigator (traps, world hazards): anything with attributes is a target.
	if (IsValid(ProjectileInstigator) && !UGBCombatLibrary::AreHostile(ProjectileInstigator, Other))
	{
		return false;
	}

	for (const TWeakObjectPtr<AActor>& Damaged : DamagedActors)
	{
		if (Damaged.Get() == Other)
		{
			return false;
		}
	}
	return true;
}

void AGBProjectile::HitTarget(AActor* Other, const FVector& ImpactPoint)
{
	DamagedActors.Add(Other);

	if (Config.ExplosionRadius > 0.f)
	{
		Explode(ImpactPoint);
	}
	else
	{
		FGBDamageInfo Info = Config.Damage;
		Info.Causer = this;
		Info.HitLocation = ImpactPoint;
		Info.HitDirection = Movement->Velocity.GetSafeNormal();
		if (const AGBEnemyCharacter* Enemy = Cast<AGBEnemyCharacter>(Other))
		{
			Info.bHeadshot = Enemy->IsHeadshotLocation(ImpactPoint);
		}
		UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(Other, Info);
	}

	if (!Config.bPierce)
	{
		Finish(Other, ImpactPoint);
	}
}

void AGBProjectile::OnCollisionOverlap(UPrimitiveComponent* OverlappedComponent, AActor* OtherActor, UPrimitiveComponent* OtherComp,
	int32 OtherBodyIndex, bool bFromSweep, const FHitResult& SweepResult)
{
	if (bFinished || !IsValidTarget(OtherActor))
	{
		return;
	}

	const FVector ImpactPoint = (bFromSweep && !SweepResult.bStartPenetrating) ? FVector(SweepResult.ImpactPoint) : GetActorLocation();
	HitTarget(OtherActor, ImpactPoint);
}

void AGBProjectile::OnCollisionHit(UPrimitiveComponent* HitComponent, AActor* OtherActor, UPrimitiveComponent* OtherComp,
	FVector NormalImpulse, const FHitResult& Hit)
{
	if (bFinished)
	{
		return;
	}

	const FVector ImpactPoint = Hit.ImpactPoint;

	if (IsValidTarget(OtherActor))
	{
		HitTarget(OtherActor, ImpactPoint);
		return;
	}

	// The world: grenades keep bouncing until the fuse ends.
	if (Config.FuseTime > 0.f)
	{
		return;
	}

	if (Config.ExplosionRadius > 0.f)
	{
		Explode(ImpactPoint);
	}
	Finish(OtherActor, ImpactPoint);
}

void AGBProjectile::Explode(const FVector& Origin)
{
	FGBDamageInfo Info = Config.Damage;
	Info.Causer = this;
	UGBCombatLibrary::DealRadialDamage(this, Origin, Config.ExplosionRadius, Info, TConstArrayView<AActor*>(), Config.bExplosionFalloff);
}

void AGBProjectile::Finish(AActor* HitActor, const FVector& Location)
{
	if (bFinished)
	{
		return;
	}
	bFinished = true;

	if (Movement)
	{
		Movement->StopMovementImmediately();
	}
	OnImpact.Broadcast(this, HitActor, Location);
	Destroy();
}
#include "Combat/GBStatusEffectComponent.h"

#include "Combat/GBCombatLibrary.h"
#include "Data/GBDataSubsystem.h"
#include "Stats/GBAttributeComponent.h"
#include "Stats/GBStatsComponent.h"
#include "GameFramework/Actor.h"

namespace
{
	// Float slack for timers so a tick that lands exactly on the end of the effect still runs.
	constexpr float GBEffectTimeEpsilon = 1.e-4f;

	const FGBStatusEffectDefinition* FindDefinition(const UObject* Context, FName EffectId)
	{
		const UGBDataSubsystem* Data = UGBDataSubsystem::Get(Context);
		return Data ? Data->FindStatusEffect(EffectId) : nullptr;
	}
}

UGBStatusEffectComponent::UGBStatusEffectComponent()
{
	PrimaryComponentTick.bCanEverTick = true;
	PrimaryComponentTick.bStartWithTickEnabled = true;
}

UGBStatusEffectComponent* UGBStatusEffectComponent::Find(const AActor* Actor)
{
	return Actor ? Actor->FindComponentByClass<UGBStatusEffectComponent>() : nullptr;
}

void UGBStatusEffectComponent::BeginPlay()
{
	Super::BeginPlay();

	if (UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(GetOwner()))
	{
		Attributes->OnDeath.AddUObject(this, &UGBStatusEffectComponent::HandleOwnerDeath);
	}
}

void UGBStatusEffectComponent::HandleOwnerDeath(const FGBDamageInfo& KillingBlow)
{
	RemoveAllEffects();
}

bool UGBStatusEffectComponent::ApplyEffect(FName EffectId, AActor* Instigator, float DurationScale, float StrengthScale)
{
	const FGBStatusEffectDefinition* FoundDefinition = FindDefinition(this, EffectId);
	if (!FoundDefinition)
	{
		UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("StatusEffect: unknown effect id '%s'"), *EffectId.ToString());
		return false;
	}
	// Copy: applying damage below runs other systems' callbacks; never hold a definition pointer across them.
	const FGBStatusEffectDefinition Definition = *FoundDefinition;

	AActor* Owner = GetOwner();
	const UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(Owner);
	if (!Owner || (Attributes && Attributes->IsDead()))
	{
		return false;
	}

	const float Strength = FMath::Max(0.f, StrengthScale);
	const float Duration = Definition.Duration * FMath::Max(0.f, DurationScale);

	ApplyHealthAndMana(EffectId, Definition.InstantHealth * Strength, Definition.InstantMana * Strength, Definition.Element, Instigator);

	// The instant part may have killed the owner (death clears every effect).
	if (Attributes && Attributes->IsDead())
	{
		return true;
	}
	if (Duration <= 0.f)
	{
		return true;
	}

	int32 ExistingCount = 0;
	int32 FirstIndex = INDEX_NONE;
	int32 ShortestIndex = INDEX_NONE;
	for (int32 Index = 0; Index < ActiveEffects.Num(); ++Index)
	{
		if (ActiveEffects[Index].EffectId != EffectId)
		{
			continue;
		}
		++ExistingCount;
		if (FirstIndex == INDEX_NONE)
		{
			FirstIndex = Index;
		}
		if (ShortestIndex == INDEX_NONE || ActiveEffects[Index].TimeRemaining < ActiveEffects[ShortestIndex].TimeRemaining)
		{
			ShortestIndex = Index;
		}
	}

	if (ExistingCount > 0 && Definition.bRefreshOnReapply)
	{
		RefreshEntry(FirstIndex, Duration, Strength, Instigator);
		return true;
	}
	if (ExistingCount >= FMath::Max(1, Definition.MaxStacks))
	{
		// At max stacks: renew the stack closest to running out.
		RefreshEntry(ShortestIndex, Duration, Strength, Instigator);
		return true;
	}

	FGBActiveStatusEffect& Entry = ActiveEffects.AddDefaulted_GetRef();
	Entry.EffectId = EffectId;
	Entry.TimeRemaining = Duration;
	Entry.TotalDuration = Duration;
	Entry.TimeToNextTick = Definition.TickInterval;
	Entry.StrengthScale = Strength;
	Entry.Instigator = Instigator;
	Entry.ModifierSource = FName(*FString::Printf(TEXT("Effect.%s#%d"), *EffectId.ToString(), NextEffectSerial++));
	const FName ModifierSource = Entry.ModifierSource;

	UpdateStackCount(EffectId);

	if (Definition.Modifiers.Num() > 0)
	{
		if (UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(Owner))
		{
			Stats->AddModifiers(ModifierSource, Definition.Modifiers, Strength);
		}
	}

	OnEffectsChanged.Broadcast();
	return true;
}

void UGBStatusEffectComponent::RefreshEntry(int32 Index, float Duration, float StrengthScale, AActor* EffectInstigator)
{
	if (!ActiveEffects.IsValidIndex(Index))
	{
		return;
	}

	FGBActiveStatusEffect& Entry = ActiveEffects[Index];
	Entry.TimeRemaining = Duration;
	Entry.TotalDuration = Duration;
	if (EffectInstigator)
	{
		Entry.Instigator = EffectInstigator;
	}

	if (StrengthScale > Entry.StrengthScale)
	{
		Entry.StrengthScale = StrengthScale;
		const FName EffectId = Entry.EffectId;
		const FName ModifierSource = Entry.ModifierSource;
		const FGBStatusEffectDefinition* Definition = FindDefinition(this, EffectId);
		UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(GetOwner());
		if (Definition && Stats && Definition->Modifiers.Num() > 0)
		{
			// Same source name: replaces the old modifiers with the stronger ones.
			const TArray<FGBModifierDef> Modifiers = Definition->Modifiers;
			Stats->AddModifiers(ModifierSource, Modifiers, StrengthScale);
		}
	}

	OnEffectsChanged.Broadcast();
}

void UGBStatusEffectComponent::UpdateStackCount(FName EffectId)
{
	int32 Count = 0;
	for (const FGBActiveStatusEffect& Entry : ActiveEffects)
	{
		if (Entry.EffectId == EffectId)
		{
			++Count;
		}
	}
	for (FGBActiveStatusEffect& Entry : ActiveEffects)
	{
		if (Entry.EffectId == EffectId)
		{
			Entry.Stacks = Count;
		}
	}
}

void UGBStatusEffectComponent::ApplyHealthAndMana(FName EffectId, float HealthAmount, float ManaAmount, EGBElement Element, AActor* EffectInstigator)
{
	AActor* Owner = GetOwner();
	UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(Owner);
	if (!Attributes || (FMath::IsNearlyZero(HealthAmount) && FMath::IsNearlyZero(ManaAmount)))
	{
		return;
	}

	if (HealthAmount > 0.f)
	{
		Attributes->Heal(HealthAmount);
	}
	else if (HealthAmount < 0.f)
	{
		FGBDamageInfo Info;
		Info.Amount = -HealthAmount;
		Info.Element = Element;
		Info.Instigator = IsValid(EffectInstigator) ? EffectInstigator : nullptr;
		Info.HitLocation = Owner->GetActorLocation();
		Info.SourceId = EffectId;
		// OnHitEffect stays None: periodic damage must not re-apply effects.
		UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(Owner, Info, /*bAllowFriendlyFire*/ true);
	}

	if (Attributes->IsDead())
	{
		return;
	}

	if (ManaAmount > 0.f)
	{
		Attributes->RestoreMana(ManaAmount);
	}
	else if (ManaAmount < 0.f)
	{
		const float Drain = FMath::Min(-ManaAmount, Attributes->GetMana());
		if (Drain > 0.f)
		{
			Attributes->SpendMana(Drain);
		}
	}
}

void UGBStatusEffectComponent::RemoveEffect(FName EffectId)
{
	UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(GetOwner());
	bool bRemoved = false;
	for (int32 Index = ActiveEffects.Num() - 1; Index >= 0; --Index)
	{
		if (ActiveEffects[Index].EffectId != EffectId)
		{
			continue;
		}
		const FName ModifierSource = ActiveEffects[Index].ModifierSource;
		ActiveEffects.RemoveAt(Index);
		if (Stats)
		{
			Stats->RemoveModifiers(ModifierSource);
		}
		bRemoved = true;
	}

	if (bRemoved)
	{
		OnEffectsChanged.Broadcast();
	}
}

void UGBStatusEffectComponent::RemoveAllEffects()
{
	if (ActiveEffects.Num() == 0)
	{
		return;
	}

	TArray<FGBActiveStatusEffect> Removed = MoveTemp(ActiveEffects);
	ActiveEffects.Reset();

	if (UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(GetOwner()))
	{
		for (const FGBActiveStatusEffect& Entry : Removed)
		{
			Stats->RemoveModifiers(Entry.ModifierSource);
		}
	}

	OnEffectsChanged.Broadcast();
}

bool UGBStatusEffectComponent::HasEffect(FName EffectId) const
{
	for (const FGBActiveStatusEffect& Entry : ActiveEffects)
	{
		if (Entry.EffectId == EffectId)
		{
			return true;
		}
	}
	return false;
}

float UGBStatusEffectComponent::GetTimeRemaining(FName EffectId) const
{
	float Remaining = 0.f;
	for (const FGBActiveStatusEffect& Entry : ActiveEffects)
	{
		if (Entry.EffectId == EffectId)
		{
			Remaining = FMath::Max(Remaining, Entry.TimeRemaining);
		}
	}
	return Remaining;
}

void UGBStatusEffectComponent::TickComponent(float DeltaTime, ELevelTick TickType, FActorComponentTickFunction* ThisTickFunction)
{
	Super::TickComponent(DeltaTime, TickType, ThisTickFunction);

	if (ActiveEffects.Num() == 0)
	{
		return;
	}

	const UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(GetOwner());
	if (Attributes && Attributes->IsDead())
	{
		return;
	}

	struct FPendingTick
	{
		FName EffectId;
		float Health = 0.f;
		float Mana = 0.f;
		EGBElement Element = EGBElement::Physical;
		TWeakObjectPtr<AActor> Instigator;
	};

	// Phase 1: advance timers only (no callbacks), so the array is not changed while we walk it.
	TArray<FPendingTick> PendingTicks;
	TArray<int32> ExpiredIndices;
	for (int32 Index = 0; Index < ActiveEffects.Num(); ++Index)
	{
		FGBActiveStatusEffect& Entry = ActiveEffects[Index];
		const FGBStatusEffectDefinition* Definition = FindDefinition(this, Entry.EffectId);
		if (!Definition)
		{
			UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("StatusEffect: effect '%s' is no longer in the data; removing it"), *Entry.EffectId.ToString());
			ExpiredIndices.Add(Index);
			continue;
		}

		// Ticks only count time inside the effect, so none run after its end.
		const float ActiveStep = FMath::Min(DeltaTime, FMath::Max(0.f, Entry.TimeRemaining));
		if (Definition->TickInterval > 0.f)
		{
			Entry.TimeToNextTick -= ActiveStep;
			while (Entry.TimeToNextTick <= GBEffectTimeEpsilon)
			{
				Entry.TimeToNextTick += Definition->TickInterval;
				FPendingTick& Pending = PendingTicks.AddDefaulted_GetRef();
				Pending.EffectId = Entry.EffectId;
				Pending.Health = Definition->HealthPerTick * Entry.StrengthScale;
				Pending.Mana = Definition->ManaPerTick * Entry.StrengthScale;
				Pending.Element = Definition->Element;
				Pending.Instigator = Entry.Instigator;
			}
		}

		Entry.TimeRemaining -= DeltaTime;
		if (Entry.TimeRemaining <= GBEffectTimeEpsilon)
		{
			ExpiredIndices.Add(Index);
		}
	}

	// Phase 2: remove expired entries and their modifiers.
	if (ExpiredIndices.Num() > 0)
	{
		UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(GetOwner());
		TArray<FName> ChangedIds;
		for (int32 i = ExpiredIndices.Num() - 1; i >= 0; --i)
		{
			const int32 Index = ExpiredIndices[i];
			const FName ModifierSource = ActiveEffects[Index].ModifierSource;
			ChangedIds.AddUnique(ActiveEffects[Index].EffectId);
			ActiveEffects.RemoveAt(Index);
			if (Stats)
			{
				Stats->RemoveModifiers(ModifierSource);
			}
		}
		for (const FName& EffectId : ChangedIds)
		{
			UpdateStackCount(EffectId);
		}
		OnEffectsChanged.Broadcast();
	}

	// Phase 3: run the ticks (they may damage, kill and clear everything).
	for (const FPendingTick& Pending : PendingTicks)
	{
		if (Attributes && Attributes->IsDead())
		{
			break;
		}
		ApplyHealthAndMana(Pending.EffectId, Pending.Health, Pending.Mana, Pending.Element, Pending.Instigator.Get());
	}
}
